My body just feels slow. Even walking to the corner store feels like it takes more out of me than it should, and sorting papers on my desk feels harder than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your symptoms significantly worsened?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. It’s been low for a while, but I can’t point to one clear period where it suddenly got much worse. It sort of comes and goes a bit, but nothing specific stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy doing things that you normally find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Not really. Most days lately I end up just sitting at the kitchen table, and it feels like the color’s gone out of everything. Even my old watercolor books… I used to enjoy looking through them, but now they just make me feel heavy instead of interested.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt completely unable to take care of yourself or perform daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Not completely unable, no. But I’ve been struggling with things I normally keep up with. I’ve let lecture plans, travel notes, and even simple household paperwork pile up because I just can’t organize myself reliably the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt somewhat better, or has the low mood been constant?\n\nPatient: It’s been pretty constant, I think. I might have a moment here or there where I’m distracted, but most days I’m just sitting at the kitchen table feeling like everything’s gone flat. Even looking at my old watercolor books just feels heavy now, not enjoyable.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because my mind’s been feeling really foggy. I work with cataloguing notes, and lately I can’t even keep my place in them like I used to. Even deciding what simple thing to do next feels harder than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days lately I’ve been feeling pretty down. I’ll sit at the kitchen table and it’s like the color’s gone out of everything. Even my old watercolor books, which I used to enjoy, just make me feel heavy now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really thought about that in any clear way. I’ve just been feeling low, but I’m not sure about thoughts of death or suicide.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling down like this?\n\nPatient: It’s been about three months, I’d say. Most days it’s been there—the low mood and that foggy feeling. It started around when I was getting ready to leave the library.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes. I used to like looking through my watercolor books, but lately I just sit there and they don’t do anything for me. It feels like the interest is gone… like everything’s kind of lost its color.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns over the past three months?\n\nPatient: My appetite has definitely changed. I have to remind myself to eat sometimes, which isn’t like me. My wife has noticed too—she’ll point out that I leave my rice or soup half-finished, and before I wouldn’t waste a meal like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. My body just feels slow. Even walking to the corner store feels like it takes more out of me than it should, and sorting papers on my desk feels harder than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your symptoms significantly worsened?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. It’s been low for a while, but I can’t point to one clear period where it suddenly got much worse. It sort of comes and goes a bit, but nothing specific stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy doing things that you normally find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Not really. Most days lately I end up just sitting at the kitchen table, and it feels like the color’s gone out of everything. Even my old watercolor books… I used to enjoy looking through them, but now they just make me feel heavy instead of interested.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt completely unable to take care of yourself or perform daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Not completely unable, no. But I’ve been struggling with things I normally keep up with. I’ve let lecture plans, travel notes, and even simple household paperwork pile up because I just can’t organize myself reliably the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt somewhat better, or has the low mood been constant?\n\nPatient: It’s been pretty constant, I think. I might have a moment here or there where I’m distracted, but most days I’m just sitting at the kitchen table feeling like everything’s gone flat. Even looking at my old watercolor books just feels heavy now, not enjoyable.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in doing?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Most days I just end up sitting at the kitchen table, and it feels like the color’s gone out of things. Even my old watercolor books don’t really interest me now—they just make me feel heavy.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating or making decisions that is noticeable to others?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s actually part of why I came in. My mind feels foggy, and I lose my place even in my old cataloguing notes. I’ll sit there and not be able to decide what simple thing to do next. I think it would be noticeable, yes.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still look forward to or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I know I’ve said it a bit already, but most days I just sit at the kitchen table and everything feels kind of drained of color. Even the watercolor books I used to like just feel heavy to look at, not something I look forward to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety, or is it more like you're moving slowly and it's an effort to do things?\n\nPatient: It’s more like I’m moving slowly. My body just feels heavy, like even walking to the corner store or sorting papers on my desk takes more effort than it should. I don’t feel especially fidgety—just slowed down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past month where you were unable to get out of bed due to feeling down?\n\nPatient: I’ve had days where I mostly stay at the kitchen table and can’t get myself moving because everything feels so flat. It’s like the color’s gone out of everything. Even things I used to like, like my watercolor books, just feel heavy to look at.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I’ve just been feeling low, but I’m not sure about thoughts of death or suicide.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling down like this?\n\nPatient: It’s been about three months, I’d say. I used to like looking through my watercolor books, but lately I just sit there and they don’t do anything for me. My body just feels slow. Even walking to the corner store feels like it takes more out of me than it should, and sorting papers on my desk feels harder than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your symptoms significantly worsened?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure.
